# Skills · O · part 67

770 entries, opus-motion-lab to oracle-bridge. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **opus-motion-lab**: Studio-grade code-driven motion design & audio-visual QA playbook distilled from Florence's Opus 5.5 Motion Lab. Enforces the 8-field Directed Motion Prompt spec, closed-form stateless spring(t, zeta, f), Motion Hierarchy (1 hero + 50-70% secondary + &lt;5% ambient), ?t= URL frame freeze, Onion-Skin & Speed-Graph diagnostics, Dual-PMREM / 180-deg shutter 3D loops, and +1-frame cosine-crest / phone-band / -2.2 dBTP audio mastering. Use when building launch videos, motion-graphic loops, 3D product shots, kinetic typography, or code-rendered animations. ([HaydernCenterpoint/Claude-Skills](https://github.com/HaydernCenterpoint/Claude-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/opus-motion-lab))
- **opus-mt-eval**: Evaluates the translation quality of OPUS-MT models across diverse language pairs using standard automatic metrics and specialized linguistic test suites. It probes general-purpose translation capability, lexical ambiguity disambiguation, and cross-lingual robustness. Use when the user wants to benchmark on Flores, Tatoeba, MuCoW, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports BLEU. - **opus-operational-safety-reviewer**: Use when a diff touches configuration, environment variables, feature flags, tsconfig, eslint config, or build/runtime config files. Distinct from opus-security-reviewer (secrets, PATs, credentials, network behaviour — not config semantics) and from opus-code-quality-reviewer (general maintainability, not ops risk). Use alongside opus-test-reviewer to confirm guardrails fire as expected.
